Completed Phase 1 work so far:

- Removed active `mysql_*` usage and added config.local support, guarded sessions, local PHP/MySQL dev runtimes, login password-hash migration, PHP 8.2+ compatibility bridge, CSRF helpers, and several admin protection batches.
- Protected section/category/subcategory create/update endpoints plus `post_file.php` and `post_ftp.php`; added upload validation helpers; hardened upload extension/MIME/size/destination/name handling; and started prepared insert cleanup for section/category/subcategory create endpoints.
- Protected `insert_advanced.php`, `update_advanced.php`, `insert_content.php`, and `update_content.php` with `red_require_admin(true)` and minimal empty-payload guards.
- Protected `insert_gallery.php`, `update_gallery.php`, `insert_form.php`, `update_form.php`, `update_main_menu.php`, and `update_sub_menu.php` with `red_require_admin(true)` and minimal empty/no-op payload guards; replaced PHP 8-incompatible `each()` loops in touched form/menu handlers.
- Protected `update_feature_slider.php` and `update_feature_template.php` with `red_require_admin(true)`.
- Tightened `update_section.php`, `update_category.php`, and `update_subcategory.php` with minimal empty/no-op payload guards, initialized rename/update state variables, removed an obsolete commented PHP `each()` block from `update_section.php`, and later replaced their raw dynamic write SQL with allowlisted prepared helpers.

Recent prepared-write batches:

- Content handlers: added `includes/admin_article_helpers.php`, replaced legacy string-built article writes in `admin/bin/insert_content.php` and `admin/bin/update_content.php` with allowlisted prepared insert/update helpers, normalized aliases/tags, converted zero/empty dates to strict-safe values, preserved article `HomeFeature`/position behavior, and allowed existing component values including `Article`, `Other`, `Gallery`, `Form`, `MainMenu`, and `SubMenu`.
- Gallery handlers: added `includes/admin_gallery_helpers.php`, replaced legacy string-built writes in `admin/bin/insert_gallery.php` and `admin/bin/update_gallery.php` with prepared multi-table writes for `RED_Articles` and `RED_C_Gallery`, normalized aliases/tags/dates, preserved gallery photo-list/delete handling, kept gallery `NewWindow` separate from the article shell, and added a `RecordID`/`RefID` pairing guard so a gallery row cannot be updated through a mismatched article id.
- Gallery UI compatibility: updated `admin/bin/edit_gallery.php` so the edit form treats the new single `yes` success response as success while still tolerating legacy response strings.
- Form handlers: added `includes/admin_form_helpers.php`, replaced legacy string-built writes in `admin/bin/insert_form.php` and `admin/bin/update_form.php` with prepared multi-table writes for `RED_Articles` and `RED_C_Form`, normalized aliases/tags through shared article helpers, preserved register-form table creation with allowlisted table/field identifiers, and added a `RecordID`/`RefID` pairing guard so a form row cannot be updated through a mismatched article id.
- Menu handlers: added `includes/admin_menu_helpers.php`, replaced legacy string-built writes in `admin/bin/update_main_menu.php` and `admin/bin/update_sub_menu.php` with allowlisted prepared helpers, preserved public table names and existing POST field names, and kept empty/no-op requests returning `no`.
- Feature toggle handlers: added `includes/admin_feature_helpers.php`, replaced legacy string-built dynamic-column updates in `admin/bin/update_feature_slider.php` and `admin/bin/update_feature_template.php` with allowlisted feature columns plus prepared `RecordID` updates, preserved feature-list ordering behavior, and fixed the helper name collision with the existing `red_admin_feature_list()` helper by using `red_admin_feature_toggle_list()`.
- Admin tool/delete/layout handlers: added `includes/admin_tool_helpers.php`, replaced `delete_label.php` raw deletes with allowlisted prepared deletes plus component/article `RecordID`/`RefID` pairing guards, replaced duplicated `run_tool_movecontent.php` and `run_tool_filterareas.php` dynamic article updates with allowlisted prepared helpers, preserved filter dash-clearing behavior, and replaced `update_layout.php` string-built layout updates with prepared updates against allowed RED tables.
- Advanced handlers: added `includes/admin_advanced_helpers.php`, replaced `insert_advanced.php` language creation with prepared fixed-item inserts, replaced `update_advanced.php` dynamic request-name SQL with explicit `Content` updates, constrained CSS reload/save targets to existing `.css` files under `/css`, preserved duplicate-language `error` behavior, and updated `admin/class/class_new_advanced.php` to treat the new single `yes` success response as success while tolerating the legacy concatenated `yes` responses.
- Area update handlers: extended `includes/admin_area_helpers.php` with shared scalar/text normalization, current-language lookup, dynamic prepared bind helpers, allowlisted area update helpers, and prepared rename-cascade helpers for `RED_Articles`, `RED_Menu`, and `RED_C_Menu`; replaced raw string-built writes in `admin/bin/update_section.php`, `admin/bin/update_category.php`, and `admin/bin/update_subcategory.php` with those helpers while preserving legacy `yes`, `updateyes`, `updateupdateyes`, `updateupdateupdateyes`, `error`, `error2`, and `error3` response shapes.
- Area edit render handlers: extended `includes/admin_area_helpers.php` with shared prepared/read helpers for area record lookup, layout/feature option loading, related-article counts, and HTML escaping; replaced raw request-interpolated reads in `admin/bin/edit_section.php`, `admin/bin/edit_category.php`, and `admin/bin/edit_subcategory.php`; added direct `red_require_admin()` guards; cast `RecordID` to integers; returned clean `no` for missing/invalid records; and preserved the existing rendered form IDs/JavaScript update/delete behavior.
- Article/form/gallery/other edit render handlers: extended article/form/gallery helpers with full prepared render lookups, layout-position lookup, position-column allowlisting, and form/gallery component `RecordID`/`RefID` pairing guards; replaced request-interpolated article/component/layout reads in `admin/bin/edit_article.php`, `admin/bin/edit_form.php`, `admin/bin/edit_gallery.php`, and `admin/bin/edit_other.php`; added direct `red_require_admin()` guards; cast `RecordID`/`ArtRecordID` to integers; returned clean `no` for invalid IDs, mismatched component/article pairs, invalid position columns, and missing records; and preserved existing rendered form IDs/hidden IDs.
- Article/form/gallery/other edit render polish: replaced the remaining legacy static option-list queries in `admin/bin/edit_article.php`, `admin/bin/edit_form.php`, `admin/bin/edit_gallery.php`, and `admin/bin/edit_other.php` with shared prepared option helpers; escaped rendered input, textarea, image filename, and link values; added hidden CSRF fields to edit save forms; tokenized edit-upload `post_file.php` URLs and `delete_label.php` AJAX payloads; removed stale LinkNavigator comment fragments; and preserved existing form IDs/hidden IDs plus gallery `NewWindow` state.
- Advanced/feature edit render handlers: extended `includes/admin_advanced_helpers.php` and `includes/admin_feature_helpers.php` with prepared render lookups, CSS file allowlisting/listing, feature-column allowlisting, component group lookup, component `RecordID` lookup through allowed component tables, and HTML escaping; replaced request-interpolated reads in `admin/bin/edit_advanced.php`, `admin/bin/edit_feature_slider.php`, and `admin/bin/edit_feature_template.php`; added direct `red_require_admin()` guards; returned clean `no` for invalid records/feature columns/languages; propagated CSRF fields/tokens for the protected save, CSS reload, and logo upload paths; and preserved existing rendered form IDs/JavaScript update behavior.
- Main menu edit render handler: extended `includes/admin_menu_helpers.php` with prepared render/read helpers for main-menu title/items/children and generated page-link options; replaced raw menu/link queries and repeated per-query database connections in `admin/bin/edit_main_menu.php`; added a direct `red_require_admin()` guard; escaped rendered menu values; propagated CSRF into the protected save form and delete-label AJAX path; and preserved existing field names/form IDs/update/delete JavaScript behavior.
- Tool render handlers: extended `includes/admin_tool_helpers.php` with prepared/read helpers for layout positions, area/layout options, active area/article dropdowns, tool article lists, allowlisted sort/position columns, and component authorization/component-record lookup; replaced request-interpolated reads in `admin/bin/tool_movecontent.php` and `admin/bin/tool_filterareas.php`; added direct `red_require_admin()` guards; propagated CSRF into the protected run-tool forms; encoded generated AJAX parameters; and added PHP 8-compatible `$This` initialization for the legacy renderer variables.
- New content/gallery/form/other/FTP render handlers: extended `includes/admin_article_helpers.php` with shared prepared fetch, option-list, gallery-type, and form-template helpers; added direct `red_require_admin()` guards to `admin/bin/new_article.php`, `admin/bin/new_gallery.php`, `admin/bin/new_form.php`, `admin/bin/new_other.php`, and `admin/bin/new_ftp.php`; normalized POST context values; allowlisted dynamic position field names; replaced request-interpolated layout/component/link-navigator/area/article option reads; propagated CSRF into save forms and `post_file.php`/`post_ftp.php` upload URLs; escaped rendered hidden admin values; and removed stale gallery comments/undefined `$row` output.
- Admin class grid/list renderers: extended `includes/admin_area_helpers.php`, `includes/admin_advanced_helpers.php`, and `includes/admin_tool_helpers.php` with prepared multi-row/list helpers, route-safe component/tool identifiers, and prepared component/tool button lookups; added direct `red_require_admin()` guards to `admin/class/class_edit_section.php`, `admin/class/class_edit_category.php`, `admin/class/class_edit_subcategory.php`, `admin/class/class_edit_advanced.php`, `admin/class/class_edit_hiddenarticles.php`, `admin/class/class_add_menu.php`, and `admin/class/class_add_tools.php`; replaced area/advanced/admin-component/tool list reads with prepared helpers; escaped rendered row values; changed add/tool AJAX payload generation to object data; and replaced the inactive-article authorization loop with the existing allowlisted component-access helper while preserving grouped component `ArtRecordID` behavior.
- Admin class static option/form renderers: added direct `red_require_admin()` guards to `admin/class/class_new_section.php`, `admin/class/class_new_category.php`, `admin/class/class_new_subcategory.php`, and `admin/class/class_edit_layout.php`; reused `red_admin_area_layouts()` and `red_admin_area_features()` for layout/feature option lists; removed the remaining active `$db->query()` calls from `admin/class`; escaped layout selector hidden context values and new-area hidden language values; and kept the existing form IDs, field names, and AJAX behavior.
- Admin class guard audit: added the missing direct `red_require_admin()` guard to `admin/class/class_new_advanced.php`, propagated a hidden CSRF field into its `insert_advanced` form, and confirmed the focused direct-access guard scan is now clean for `admin/bin` and `admin/class`.
- Public page chrome/layout render batch: added `includes/public_render_helpers.php` with prepared public fetch helpers, RED table/column allowlists, route-based area/article lookups, public HTML/title/meta helpers, and advanced-item readers; replaced raw public reads in `includes/header.php`, `includes/footer.php`, `class/class_layout.php`, `class/class_limit.php`, `class/class_metatags.php`, and `class/class_pagetitle.php`; escaped title/meta attribute output while preserving stored CMS HTML for header/footer content; and fixed CMS entity handling so title/meta text no longer double-encodes entities such as `&eacute;`.
- Public content/menu render batch: extended `includes/public_render_helpers.php` with allowlisted article position/order helpers, route-derived prepared article list filters, and prepared menu/root readers; replaced raw public/admin renderer reads in `class/class_content.php` and `class/class_main_menu.php`; preserved component dispatch, public menu hierarchy, admin `cp_menu()` form IDs, and `cp_articles()` order forms; escaped public menu labels/links and the admin main-menu edit trigger values.
- Public breadcrumb/feature render batch: extended `includes/public_render_helpers.php` with prepared breadcrumb title lookups, area feature checks, and allowlisted feature-article list helpers; replaced raw public/admin reads in `class/class_build_breadcrumb.php`, `class/class_feature_slider.php`, and `class/class_feature_template.php`; escaped breadcrumb labels/links, slider/template image/link attributes, and feature edit-form hidden context values; preserved the public slider output, admin `cp_slider()` edit form, and CSRF propagation into feature edit requests.
- Public component render batch: extended `includes/public_render_helpers.php` with prepared article/component row lookups, layout dimension helpers matching the existing `RED_Layouts` schema, admin component authorization helpers, JavaScript identifier normalization, and decoded-safe display text escaping; replaced raw public/admin component reads in `class/class_article.php`, `class/class_gallery.php`, `class/class_forms.php`, `class/class_other.php`, and `class/class_component_template.php`; preserved stored CMS body HTML, public article/gallery/form rendering, and admin `cp_article`/`cp_gallery`/`cp_form`/`cp_other` edit-control forms.
- Public form submission batch: added `includes/public_form_helpers.php`, replaced public form/login endpoint `RED_C_Form` lookups in `bin/contact.php`, `bin/register.php`, `bin/response.php`, `bin/storelogin.php`, and `bin/register_storelogin.php` with prepared `RecordID` lookups, constrained CMS-generated table and field identifiers, replaced dynamic registration-table inserts and store-login/email lookups with prepared helpers, honored `MySpamTrap` even when it is appended outside the stored `LongDesc` definition, escaped submitted values before rendering email/response placeholders, and switched the public mail endpoints to the bundled namespaced PHPMailer class.

Verification from latest public PayPal callback batch:

Clear Phase 1 continuation plan:

1. Inventory the remaining admin write endpoints and classify each as single-table or multi-table. Add transactions only where multiple related writes can leave inconsistent state; do not wrap the Move/Filter tools merely because they are called tools if they execute one atomic `UPDATE`.
2. Run a Phase 1 closeout security audit across admin guards/CSRF, prepared reads and writes, upload boundaries, public submission endpoints, transaction coverage, and direct-access behavior. Fix findings in small file-family batches.
3. Re-run the clean-install acceptance suite: fresh `db-structure.sql` import, canonical table/count/engine audit, relationship checks, public routes, authenticated admin overlays, and focused create/edit/delete workflows with disposable rows.
4. Record any remaining Phase 1 risks and decide whether Phase 1 can close. Do not start Phase 2 until this acceptance pass is complete and documented.
5. Keep the remaining Phase 0 operational tasks separate: rotate real production credentials, confirm production PHP/MySQL versions, and complete local GitHub authentication/push without placing credentials in chat.

- Full active-code PHP lint passes across 110 PHP files. Guard inventory found no unguarded `admin/bin` or `admin/class` PHP files, and every `insert_*`, `update_*`, `delete_*`, `run_*`, and `post_*` admin endpoint uses `red_require_admin(true)`.
- Active-code scans found no direct request-superglobal interpolation into SQL, no direct legacy `session_start()` outside the guarded bootstrap helper, and no active `mysql_*` calls. Remaining dynamic SQL is confined to prepared helpers with allowlisted table/column identifiers; `class_connection.php` retains its legacy generic mysqli wrapper for compatibility but active migrated paths no longer call raw `$db->query()` methods.
- The first fresh installer import exposed one malformed quote/comma sequence in the sanitized `RED_Admin` seed left by the Audio Player component-list removal. Corrected that single installer seed syntax blocker and reran the complete suite.
- A fresh disposable `redcms_phase1_acceptance` database imported `db-structure.sql` and all five migrations successfully. Rerunning every migration also succeeded. The resulting schema has 14 canonical tables, 9 InnoDB transaction tables, 5 articles, 2 forms, 1 gallery, 2 menu rows, 3 sections, 19 components, 2 sanitized admin seeds, zero retired Audio Player/Store components, and zero missing section/form/gallery relationships.
- Normalized schema-only dumps of the clean acceptance database and live `redcms_dev` were identical after ignoring auto-increment counters.
- Clean-install runtime acceptance on an isolated server returned non-empty HTTP 200 responses for `/` and `/contacto/` with no runtime-error markers. A disposable plaintext admin logged in successfully, upgraded to a 60-character bcrypt hash, rendered the authenticated overlay, add-article form, and edit-article form, and exposed CSRF tokens.
- Clean-install guards returned `HTTP 403 no` without a session and `HTTP 403 csrf` for an authenticated invalid token. A tokened impossible order update returned HTTP 200 `no`. Logout destroyed the admin session and removed overlay markers.
- Removed the disposable admin/database, revoked its temporary application-account grant, stopped isolated acceptance servers, and verified live `redcms_dev` remains at 14 tables/5 articles with zero `codex-*` admins, high-ID smoke articles, or disposable advanced languages.
- The only global `git diff --check` output remains the previously documented line-ending/trailing-whitespace condition in modified `css/forms.css`; no closeout batch touched that file, and all scoped checks pass.
- Decision: Phase 1 is complete. The CMS meets the Phase 1 runtime, configuration, authentication, request protection, upload, input/SQL, transaction, clean-installer, and compatibility goals without changing public URLs or database table names.

Remaining work outside Phase 1:

1. Phase 0 operations remain open: rotate real production credentials, confirm production PHP/MySQL versions, and complete GitHub authentication/push.
2. Decide explicitly whether and when to begin Phase 2. Do not begin Phase 2 automatically.

Phase 1 closeout handoff (superseded by the Phase 2 decision below):

- At closeout, Phase 1 was complete and Phase 2 had not yet started. The user subsequently authorized the Administrator Users feature below as the first Phase 2 addition.

Phase 2 — Administrator Users:

- Added an owner-only `Administrator Users` entry as the first row of the existing Advanced list, above Website Title. It opens through the same `#msggbox_edit_advanced` target and renders inside `article.grid_12.cp_admin`.
- Added a protected account manager for creating, updating, password-resetting, and deleting administrator accounts. The manager now opens to two compact choices: Add User loads only the new-user form; Edit User loads an email list, and selecting an email loads only that account form. This avoids rendering every account form on one long page.
- Email is required and application-unique for every new or updated account, with storage widened to `varchar(254)`. The two legacy accounts still have blank email values and appear as `Email required` repair rows; their addresses were not invented, and either account must receive a valid email before it can be saved.
- New passwords require at least 12 characters, are stored with `password_hash()`, are never rendered, and duplicate usernames are rejected.
- Existing `RED_Admin.AdminComponents` permissions are editable per user. Added `RED_Admin.AdminTools` for explicit access to `RED_Tools`, defaulting existing accounts to the two current tools for compatibility.
- Added utility-tool checks to both tool render endpoints and both write endpoints, so hiding a tool is backed by server-side authorization.
- Login now stores the administrator record ID, username, utility permissions, and a password fingerprint. Every protected admin request refreshes permissions from `RED_Admin`; deleted accounts and password-reset sessions are rejected immediately.
- Only `webmaster`/`superadmin` accounts can see or use Administrator Users. Self-deletion and deletion of the final manager are blocked.
- Added `database/migrations/2026-07-10-red-admin-user-tools.sql`, aligned `db-structure.sql`, and applied the migration to `redcms_dev`. The two original accounts remain bcrypt-hashed and retain both current utility tools.
- Focused HTTP smokes passed for unauthenticated and CSRF rejection, create/hash storage, restricted menu visibility, server-side tool denial, permission update, password reset, old-session rejection, self-delete rejection, delete, and deleted-session rejection. Temporary administrator rows were removed.
- Headless Chrome verified the actual Advanced accordion click path at 1440x1000 and 390x844: the default view has exactly two choices and zero forms; Add User has one form with required 254-character email storage; Edit User has only the email rows and zero forms; selecting an email loads exactly one form. Both viewports had no horizontal overflow, console errors, or failed requests.

Next Phase 2 starting point:

- Have the owner fill the two legacy missing emails, then test Add User and Edit User with real accounts. A later optional enhancement can add expiring email invitations; the current version creates the account immediately and expects credentials to be shared securely out of band.

Phase 1 security extension — multi-user authorization:

- Preserved the reliability and template milestones in `docs/ROADMAP.md`. The next milestone is Phase 2 database stabilization; the template contract and cosmetic/admin work remain later milestones.

- Site layout, main navigation, sections, categories, subcategories, advanced settings, language creation, and administrator-account management are limited to `webmaster` and `superadmin`. Guest overlays do not render those panels, while the corresponding endpoints return HTTP 403 `no`.
- Minimal authorization-denial events are written to the server error log with administrator ID, request method, and endpoint; no password, token, or content body is logged.
- A disposable Video-only guest and disposable owner were used for adversarial testing. The guest received HTTP 403 `no` for Gallery, Contact Form, Article edit/insert/delete/order, cross-subtype changes, unauthorized bulk records, FTP, upload, layout, and Advanced requests. Video new/edit returned HTTP 200. The owner retained all 12 content choices and owner panels.
- Database verification confirmed zero rows from denied insert/upload tests, the original Video remained `GalleryType=Video`, the original article component remained `Gallery`, and the protected Instructions row remained present.
- Headless Chrome at 1440x1000 and 390x844 confirmed the guest sees exactly one Add Content choice (`Video`), can open one Video form, sees no structure/Advanced/user-management controls, and has no overflow, console errors, or failed requests. Owner desktop verification retained all 12 content choices and every management panel.
- Added the Account Type selector to both Administrator Users forms, then simplified the assignable model to Guest and Webmaster because Superadmin duplicated Webmaster behavior. Legacy `superadmin` database values remain recognized internally for compatibility, but form submissions and direct requests cannot assign new Superadmin accounts.
- Role smoke tests create hashed Guest and Webmaster accounts, promote Guest to Webmaster, reject `superadmin` and other invalid direct assignments, and reject a current-account role change. Desktop and mobile browser checks render exactly two options with Guest selected by default and the saved role selected on edit.

Phase 2 read-only encoding-damage audit:

- Kept this batch read-only against application data and schema. No database row, table definition, migration, or application behavior was changed.
- Confirmed the live schema has 140 character columns: 63 `latin1` columns across `RED_Admin`, `RED_Articles`, `RED_C_Form`, and `RED_C_Gallery`; 75 `utf8mb3` columns across ten registry/content-structure tables; and two `utf8mb4` columns in `RED_Schema_Migrations`.
- Scanned all 268 nonempty character cells by raw stored bytes and decoded values. The scan found zero high-byte values, zero UTF-8-as-Latin-1/mojibake or replacement-character signatures, zero suspicious control bytes, and zero double-encoded HTML entities. Every currently stored nonempty text value is 7-bit ASCII, so the canonical local data does not need a byte-repair or double-decoding pass before charset conversion.
- The only HTML entities are intentional markup in Instructions article `89196971`, in `ShortDesc` and `LongDesc`: 157 total tokens (`&amp;`, `&gt;`, `&nbsp;`, `&ndash;`, and `&trade;`). No `&amp;...;` double-entity pattern exists. The rendered Instructions page preserves the intended entities without mojibake.
- Confirmed the local MySQL server/database defaults and the actual PHP `connection` class session currently negotiate `utf8mb4`; client, connection, and results are all `utf8mb4`. `class/class_connection.php` does not yet call `mysqli_set_charset()`, so production behavior still depends on the server handshake default and should be made explicit during normalization.
- No active application code uses `utf8_encode()`, `utf8_decode()`, `mb_convert_encoding()`, or `iconv()` to reinterpret database content. Existing public text helpers use the intended decode-then-escape behavior for stored HTML entities.
- Public `/`, `/contacto/`, and `/administracion/instructions` returned HTTP 200 with 6,414, 10,132, and 26,977 bytes. Responses declare UTF-8 in the HTML and `Content-Type`; scans found no mojibake, replacement-character, or double-entity output.
- Conclusion: current canonical rows can proceed through direct, backup-backed `CONVERT TO CHARACTER SET utf8mb4` migrations. Preserve row counts and fingerprints and verify rendered output; do not run speculative content repair or decode/re-encode updates.
- This result covers local `redcms_dev` and the checked-in installer snapshot only. Run the same read-only byte/content scan against production immediately before deployment because production access/version confirmation remains a separate Phase 0 operational item and its data may differ.

Next Phase 2 batch:

Phase 2 failed-login throttling foundation:

- Created external rollback package `/Users/oscarrojas/Documents/red-cms-archive/phase2-login-throttling-2026-07-12/`. Database backup `pre-login-throttling-redcms_dev.sql` has SHA-256 `b98aa558e962e3c9a87df85d964530977d237b4d77107d6c32747bac1d7d00dc`; code archive `pre-login-throttling-code.tar.gz` has SHA-256 `615de6f0e5fef2360ba28e1f7383551e8dd3cf245af3ef586f0b948d13ad3e0b`.
- Baseline `redcms_dev` had 15 total tables including the migration ledger, no login-attempt storage, two canonical administrators with focused fingerprint `2818850285`, and 22 applied migrations with zero pending or drifted files.
- Added idempotent migration `database/migrations/2026-07-12-login-attempt-throttling.sql` and aligned `db-structure.sql`. New InnoDB table `RED_Login_Attempts` has only `RecordID`, binary `UsernameHash`, packed `ClientAddress`, and `AttemptedAt`, with exact username/time and client/time indexes. No foreign key is used because unknown usernames must be throttled too.
- The migration validates the four-column contract, primary key, and both index definitions. A direct rerun no-op'd safely; an intentionally malformed disposable client index was rejected through the expected schema guard rather than silently accepted.
- Added `includes/login_throttle_helpers.php` and integrated it into the existing `bin/login.php` response contract. Usernames are trimmed/lowercased then SHA-256 hashed. Client identity uses only packed `REMOTE_ADDR`; client-supplied `X-Forwarded-For` is ignored. No submitted username, password, password hash, session ID, CSRF token, content, or successful activity history is stored.
- Policy is a rolling 15-minute window: block a username/client pair at five failures, a username across clients at 15, or a client across usernames at 30. The blocked response stays HTTP 200 `no`. Blocked requests do not extend the window. A successful login clears every failure for that normalized username while retaining failures for other usernames from the same client and leaving existing authenticated sessions valid.
- Retention is 24 hours. Each eligible login removes up to 500 expired rows using the timestamp portion of the two throttle indexes. The table remains transactional; a forced fixture insert rolled back to zero rows.
- Restored database `redcms_login_throttle_restore_20260712` had exactly one pending migration, applied it in 5 ms, reran with zero pending/drifted files, produced the exact 16-table schema, and preserved the two-user fingerprint `2818850285`. Clean database `redcms_login_throttle_clean_20260712` imported the updated installer, replayed all 23 migrations, and reran with zero pending/drifted files.
- Runtime acceptance used disposable plaintext Webmaster `codex_login_throttle`. Five mixed-case wrong-password attempts entered one normalized pair bucket; the next correct password returned `no` and remained plaintext while locked. Aging the rows to 16 minutes allowed normal login, upgraded the password to a 60-character bcrypt hash, and cleared the username failures.
- Separate fixtures proved the 15-failure username-wide threshold across 15 client addresses and the 30-failure client-wide threshold across 30 username hashes. Existing authenticated access stayed HTTP 200 during a new-login lockout. A 25-hour row was removed while a current unrelated row remained. A spoofed `X-Forwarded-For` still stored packed `127.0.0.1`, confirming the trusted-boundary rule.
- Desktop Chrome submitted the real rendered `/administracion/` login form, returned to the same public URL with authenticated alias `ThrottleQA`, and showed no console errors. Mobile Chrome at 390×844 rendered the username/password/submit controls with `scrollWidth=390` and no console or failed-request errors. The desktop navigation aborted the existing external YouTube iframe during redirect; this was the only failed request and is unrelated to authentication.
- Applied the migration locally in 7 ms. Normal local login with temporary Webmaster `codex_local_throttle` returned `yes`, upgraded plaintext to bcrypt, rendered a protected admin response at HTTP 200, and left zero attempt rows after success. Deleting the fixture made its existing protected session return HTTP 403 `no`. Cleanup restored two administrators/fingerprint `2818850285` and an empty attempt table.
- Final local state is 16 InnoDB tables, 23 applied migrations with zero pending or drifted files, two canonical administrators, and zero login-attempt rows. `/`, `/contacto/`, `/administracion/`, `/administracion/instructions`, and `/administracion/test-vimeo` remain HTTP 200 with no runtime-error markers. Full PHP lint and `git diff --check` pass. Both disposable databases, grants, test accounts, and attempt fixtures were removed; their protected sessions were invalidated, and normal local PHP/MySQL services remain running.

- `/img/logo.png` is absent as a file. Its only source references are inside inactive commented example markup in the old header and the legacy theme header partial. This makes future standard-theme visuals intentionally differ from today's no-logo header and therefore requires explicit design/activation approval; it still should not copy the asset into `/img`.

- Added `includes/theme_layout_helpers.php` and converted the standard adapter, public dispatcher, control-panel wrappers, area/content/tool selectors, and position options to the active effective manifest. Blocked transitions report exact missing ids/positions and change neither theme state nor content.
- Direct area, article, order, move, and layout-tool writes now validate the effective active contract. Layout switches refuse targets that would strand existing content positions. PagePosition ownership mirrors the public route relationship, including bounded multi-reference `Article LIKE '%alias%'` behavior. Route-owner existence prevents forged wildcard layout-tool updates. HomeFeature selects the active Home layout's first declared position instead of assuming position `1`. New-item image uploads stage an inactive row at position `0` under the first effective canonical layout instead of persisting legacy `Full-Width`/position `1` defaults.
- Added one database-scoped, same-connection-reentrant theme-contract mutex. Activation holds it from the final live preflight through state transaction commit and post-commit verification; every layout/position writer holds the same mutex through validation and commit. Cross-connection writes therefore cannot create a compatibility gap between activation preflight and activation. Generic Advanced writes now bind the posted Item to the fetched language-scoped row, and logo uploads require the exact language-scoped `Website_Logo` target; neither path can mutate `System_Active_Theme` or `System_Previous_Theme` by forged RecordID.

Milestone 4 Home feature-slider completion — complete:

- The missing hero was a combination of data and renderer defects. **Features Picture** only stores an Article's candidate image; it does not assign that Article to an area's slider. Home had `Features=slider` but zero selected slider Articles. At the same time, `feature_slider::cp_slider()` returned before rendering **Edit Slider** when the selected set was empty, making the first assignment impossible. The component inventory helper also built its rows without returning them, which produced the visible `foreach(null)` warning once the selector opened. Finally, `starter-reference` rendered only `slides[0]`, and the legacy partial emitted compatibility markup rather than a working multi-slide hero.
- Restored the enabled-empty administrator path: **Edit Slider** now remains visible with zero selected Articles, and the component helper returns its bounded row array. Through the authenticated CMS UI, selected only Article `1257806882` (`demo article`) at Home slider order `1` and Article `2015783696` (`test article`) at order `2`. The resulting rows retain `sunflower.jpg` and `website-1.jpg` respectively. The current persisted theme state is `active=legacy-bootstrap`, `previous=starter-reference`; Home Section `13` is back on audited layout `index-1` with `Features=slider`.
- Both production packages now consume the entire ordered core-prepared slide array. Zero slides emit no wrapper; one slide emits no redundant controls; multiple slides render all images, titles, summaries, links, and targets. `legacy-bootstrap` uses an accessible manual Bootstrap carousel. `starter-reference` declares local production-only `assets/js/hero-slider.js` plus production CSS for previous/next buttons, direct indicators, status, Left/Right Arrow handling, a first-slide no-script fallback, responsive mobile geometry, and reduced-motion behavior. Pre-Milestone 5 Move Content correction — complete:

- Reproduced the user's exact **Content → Tools → Move Content** state in the open authenticated Chrome tab at `/about/#cp_content`: `Github link` was selected, destination Section `home` and position `1` were visible, but the database row remained unchanged. Browser inspection proved the immediate cause. `form#toolmove` contained only the source list; the destination Section/Category/SubCategory/Article selectors, Position selector, CSRF field, and Update submit control all had `form=null`. The Update button therefore had no form to submit and no endpoint request was made. There were no console errors because this was malformed legacy HTML rather than a JavaScript exception.
- A second coupled defect would have remained after repairing the form. The legacy server contract reused the current page's `VarPosition`. Moving from About to Home would therefore write `Sections=home` plus `SectionPosition`, while Home rendering requires `HomePosition`; moving back could also leave a stale Home placement. The prior transaction tests covered Section-to-Section moves only and did not exercise a change of placement context.
- The `code-change-safety-checkpoint` workflow preserved `/private/tmp/redcms-move-content-pre-20260718-01.tar.gz` before editing. Its SHA-256 is `5cb29cb1b67ae26a363613d24839e4e0458c658491cc8564cd7a4ba4a23d763f`. It is a rollback artifact only; restoration requires a separate reviewed decision.
- Corrected the fragment structure in `admin/bin/tool_movecontent.php`: `form#toolmove` now owns its fieldset and wrapper, all destination controls, the CSRF field, the source/destination position state, and Update. The two premature/extra wrapper closes were removed; no field names, tool permissions, editor actions, filtering, sorting, or legacy `yes`/`no` response shape changed.
- Added destination-aware placement selection in `includes/admin_tool_helpers.php`. The deepest selected target determines the write column in this order: Article → `PagePosition`; SubCategory → `SubCategoryPosition`; Category → `CategoryPosition`; Home Section → `HomePosition`; other Section → `SectionPosition`; no new target → current allowlisted `VarPosition`. When the source and destination contexts differ, only the source position is cleared before setting the destination position. Other intentional placements are preserved. A cross-context move without a Position fails closed. The existing `red_admin_article_update()` path validates the chosen position against the destination area's actual active-theme layout inside the existing batch transaction; Filter Areas behavior is unchanged.
- Reloaded the local page and reopened Move Content through the real Tools UI. Browser DOM now reports exactly one `toolmove` form; every destination selector, CSRF field, both allowlisted `VarPosition` controls, Layout state, and Update button are owned by it. The form is left with zero selected Articles, blank destination/position, and no console warnings/errors. No primary form was submitted.
- Expanded `scripts/dev-acceptance.sh` inside the existing disposable Section lifecycle. It now renders the real tool and checks one complete form, moves the active fixture Section → Home through `run_tool_movecontent.php` with exact state `home:1:0` and public Home marker, moves it Home → Section with exact state `codex-section-archive:0:2` and public Section marker, submits undeclared Home position `99`, requires legacy `no`, and proves the exact state remains unchanged. Milestone 5 custom Layout Builder v1 — complete:

- The primary entry is contextual: **Build** sits beside the current page's Layout selector and Map. **Advanced → Layout Builder** remains the full library, history, restore, and archive entry. It is intentionally not under Tools because Move operates on content placement while Layout Builder defines the reusable page structure that those placement tools consume.
- A Webmaster can start with one full-width row, create a blank layout, or copy any active package layout; add 12-unit row presets by click or drag; reorder rows and positions; adjust spans; name positions; and switch the live map between desktop and mobile stacking. Touch/keyboard move controls mirror drag behavior. The mobile action groups use compact two-row grids so every control remains inside its card at `375×844`.
- Layout data is bounded declarative JSON only. Stable ids use `custom-*`; every row must total exactly 12 units; positions are unique integers `1–99`; row/column/layout limits are enforced; and no PHP, HTML, CSS, query, path, or executable text is accepted. Drafts are separate from published snapshots. Every save, publish, restore, and archive creates an immutable revision and uses stale-state protection.
- Published, non-archived custom layouts merge into the active **standard** theme's catalog and are consumed by page selectors, position selectors, Move Content, Article placement validation, public rendering, compatibility, preflight, and readiness. Core renders the structure with one generic CSS Grid layer and mobile source-order stacking. Package layouts remain unchanged, and `legacy-bootstrap` remains the hard recovery renderer rather than being deleted.
- Publishing refuses to remove a position that currently contains content. Archival refuses a layout still assigned to any Section, Category, Subcategory, or Article. Restore is non-destructive and creates a new current version. Migration `2026-07-24-custom-layout-builder.sql` adds `RED_Custom_Layouts` and `RED_Custom_Layout_Revisions`; applying it to primary created the two empty support tables only and did not create, publish, assign, or move any primary content layout.
- Verification passes the focused 36-assertion custom-layout lifecycle, 283 theme/admin contracts, 67 layout-extensibility assertions, 50 readiness assertions, PHP/JavaScript syntax and whitespace checks, and the complete disposable acceptance suite with 20 InnoDB tables and 31 applied migrations. Signed-in Chrome confirms the top Build entry, Advanced library, unsaved name/id generation, row addition, desktop/mobile preview, desktop zero overflow, and mobile zero page overflow; a real clipped mobile action strip found during QA was corrected and the row now reports equal scroll/client width. No primary layout was saved or published in browser QA.
- Refreshing Adriana from the new 20-table primary schema exposed one migration-order defect: the guarded clone importer attempted to remove parent Sections before the user's now-related Subcategories and Categories. The transaction rolled back, its exact failed disposable database was verified absent, and the importer was corrected to delete Subcategories → Categories → Sections. Fresh clone `redcms_adriana_28_20260724_030810_48216` now passes the 28-route/153-section model, exact grant and process ownership, Home marker, all 42 media hashes, and protected-primary isolation at `http://127.0.0.1:8060/`.
- Recovery evidence for this batch is `/private/tmp/redcms-layout-builder-v1.kYx6Gu`. The intentionally dirty worktree was preserved; nothing was staged, committed, reset, activated, promoted, deployed, or assigned automatically.
